I wasn’t sure if that’s something worth coming in for, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been… longer than I wanted to admit, maybe about a year and three months. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, hi. I guess I’ve just been feeling really worn out most days. It’s probably not a huge thing, but even when I get a full night’s sleep, I wake up feeling like my body and my head are still kind of dragging, especially when I have to go in for my cashier shift. I wasn’t sure if that’s something worth coming in for, but it’s been happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess it’s been… longer than I wanted to admit, maybe about a year and three months. It kind of started after our older relative moved in with us. I mean, it hasn’t been awful every single day, but yeah, around then is when I started noticing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed over the past year or so?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not totally sure. I mean, I still do enjoy things when I actually get around to them, I think. Maybe I haven’t paid super close attention to whether that’s changed. It’s not like everything feels pointless or anything like that. Like, if I actually sit down and watch something I like or spend time doing something fun, I can still enjoy it. Maybe I’m a little distracted sometimes, but I wouldn’t say the pleasure is gone or anything. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling nervous or on edge a lot of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have been kind of on edge. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but my mind jumps around a lot—like money stuff, the relative staying with us, my wife being stressed, church responsibilities, and then whether I’m messing things up at work. It’s just… a lot of little things, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling consistently down or depressed most of the time over the past year?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not consistently, no. I hope that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: When you are doing things you normally enjoy, do you feel less interested in them than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I don’t think that’s a big thing for me, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having less fun or enjoying things less than you used to, even when you're doing activities that are normally pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still enjoy things when I’m doing them… watching something, spending time with someone, that kind of thing. Maybe I’m not always totally relaxed, but I wouldn’t say I’m having less fun than I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past year?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Maybe I don’t get super excited every time, but I wouldn’t say it’s less than it used to be. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or empty inside?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I have off days like anyone, but I wouldn’t say I often feel sad or empty inside. I don’t think that’s a big thing for me, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're having less fun or enjoying things less than you used to, even when you're doing activities that are normally pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I still enjoy things when I’m doing them… watching something, spending time with someone, that kind of thing. Maybe I’m not always totally relaxed, but I wouldn’t say I’m having less fun than I used to. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find it difficult to concentrate or make decisions?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah… not all the time, but it happens. Like at the register, I can be looking right at the screen and still kind of lose track because I’m thinking about stuff at home or bills. Then I have to recount the drawer or catch a small mistake. It’s not constant, but it does happen enough that I notice it. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you have days where you feel completely fine and your mood is normal?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. I mean, I do have days where my mood feels pretty normal and I’m not… down or anything. Maybe I still get a little in my head sometimes, but overall, yes, I’d say I have normal days. Is that kind of what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you had at least two years where you felt down or depressed for most of the day, even if it wasn't every single day?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I’ve had stretches where I felt stressed or kind of worn down, but not like two years of being depressed most of the day. At least, I wouldn’t describe it that way. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to push yourself to get interested in or enjoy activities that you normally like?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not really. I mean, if I’m tired I might put things off a little, but I wouldn’t say I have to force myself to enjoy things I normally like. Once I’m doing them, it’s usually fine. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not totally sure… I don’t think it’s been most of the day nearly every day for two weeks. I mean, I’ve had stretches where I feel on edge or worn out, but it’s not constant all day. I hope that answers it right.
